ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u4	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Białowieża Forest is the only Polish natural object on the UNESCO World Heritage List. It is a unique place, the last lowland natural forest in Europe, which is a refuge for many species of plants and animals that can no longer be found anywhere else. There is currently a massive logging of trees in the forest. The pretext for the current tree clipping is the periodic mass appearance of the printer bark. The idea of combating bark in the forest by increasing the cutting of trees is negatively assessed by both environmental organisations and scientists from 14 Polish universities and research institutions, including the Scientific Council of the Białowieża National Park, the National Council for Nature Conservation and the Nature Conservation Committee of the Polish Academy of Sciences. It is also disturbing not to enter the Białowieża Forest. In particular, I am referring to people who derive income from tourism in this area. The prohibition of entry into the forest has had an impact on the decline in earnings of the majority of Białowieża residents, in particular since the start of the tourist season. I have questions for the Minister in this area. Why, Minister, do you not respect the opinion of the world of science and do not allow for a substantive debate with scientists, nor do you allow scientists to enter the Białowieża Forest? They are not a threat. And I have another question: How long will the ban on entering the Białowieża Forest last? It is also worrying to increase the plan of the forest installation from 40 thousand. M? Up to 160 thousand. m? I have a question: How many trees [[♪ The bell ♪]] This plan was cut out for today? Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u16	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! The elimination of white spots, because it is really the subject of this question, is one of the priority tasks of the Ministry of Digitalization. It is implemented primarily on the basis of the Operational Programme of the Digital Poland, under which further recruitments are carried out, whereby telecommunications operators can obtain funding for the construction of broadband networks aimed primarily at residents. And the long-term objective of this action is to cover the whole of Poland in such a way that this access for all inhabitants of the Republic is ensured. Right now, we're after the second contest. We're gonna be signing contracts tomorrow with the telecommunications operators who won this contest. And on that basis, we're going to have about 60%. NUTS which were selected for the construction of the network, developed. The next will be carried out on the basis of another call, which will be carried out immediately after an analysis of the detailed situation in those areas where either operators have not reported or the operators have failed to comply with the formal requirements. As regards the issue of what is profitable, what is not profitable, this project is generally carried out for those entities which would not be interested in building the network on purely commercial terms. And that's what funding is for. The Novum that we have introduced, and which will additionally provide access to the smallest towns, is an obligation to connect all schools in the competition areas, literally all schools with 100 Mbps broadband fibre, which will result in the provision of further services by these operators, because - I will say it so simply - if they have a fiber optic lead to small towns and schools are in very different towns, then this basic skeleton will be created. In order to make it more attractive and to make this realization and connection so that this fiber can reach as many towns as possible and given the needs of modernization of the Polish school, on 13 June we made a decision - I am talking about the government - the Council of Ministers has adopted a resolution on the construction of the National Education Network - 100 Mega on the 100th anniversary of the Polish State. As part of this decision, under this resolution, the government committed itself to providing access to the Internet to all schools in Poland. It is about broadband Internet access with a capacity of at least 100 Mbps. Importantly, this service will be offered to schools free of charge. To this end, we are preparing a draft law which will be addressed to the public consultation in July this year. We assume that in September this project will go to the meeting of the High House. As I said, on the one hand, it is intended to make this project more attractive from a business point of view, because, where it is not profitable, we will ensure constant reception of the signal, that is, this investment will be profitable, but on the other hand, the most important goal for us in the case of OSE is to modernise the Polish school, to provide access to digital services at an equal level to all institutions in Poland regardless of their location. We assume that OSE will be built in the next 3 years and thus broadband Internet will be delivered to all schools in 19.5 thousand. location. It will by force create a network that can later be used for other purposes, but this is a matter of the future. Thank you very much.

This lack of use is evidenced by the fact that we have such situations that the node is located in a school that is not connected to the Internet, it is the ORSS node... I mean, the dashboard is installed at school, but this school doesn't use the Internet, it's not connected. We have a lot of situations like this. In Podlaskie Voivodeship there are about 600 schools located less than 1 km from the network and they are not connected, so it shows here that it does not work and we need to improve it. With regard to certain production purposes, which are designated and such an objective is e.g. Bringing a 3 inch gallium nitride substrate into production is yet, as I say, related to this long, long distance and requires many, many R &amp; D work. So, in summary, we are aware of the potential of Ammon. We're doing our best. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u33	Thank you very much, sir. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Minister Marek Tombarkiewicz. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u34	Ladies and gentlemen! The answer to the first question is very short. Polish patients will be able to treat themselves as they have been, on the same basis in the future. so far, i.e. to the same extent benefit from guaranteed benefits which are financed by public funds. The change proposed by us absolutely does not affect any rights of patients who may have benefits financed by the NFZ, so that this does not apply to the current situation. Everything that has happened now will continue. This proposal was intended only to equalise opportunities between public and non-public actors. The current situation is as follows: a non-public entity or a medicinal entity that is an entrepreneur may both provide those benefits which are financed by a public payer, i.e. the NFZ, and can be partly co-financed by private funds or private benefits. And if the same hospital, which is not an entrepreneur, or a SPOZ, stands next to such a healthcare provider, he cannot provide benefits for payment. After all, no one tells patients to go there and buy private benefits. This is just giving an opportunity to equalise opportunities between public and private establishments, and for patients it is also giving an opportunity to buy out certain diagnostic or therapeutic benefits in certain specific situations. This absolutely does not affect the current status of patients' privileges. On the other hand, as regards the answer to the second question, I would like to say that the financing of health care is quite precisely governed by the provisions of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare, which is a very old law, which has been amended several times. Commercial benefits provided by various medicinal entities, i.e. independent public health establishments are implemented on a voluntary basis, so no one will draw from patients, no one will demand, no one will pressure patients to buy these benefits. If the patient wishes and, as stated in the explanatory memorandum of the bill, under certain precisely defined conditions, through separate staff, separate potential, these benefits can take place in a separate period of time. So there's absolutely no talking about getting any money out of patients. According to the draft law amending the Law on medicinal activities and certain other legislations, patients will be able to benefit from paid health benefits in a public entity, if it proposes such services. It is not an obligation that every OPA will put up a price list and say: please, I want to provide these services. If the hospital's got enough potential... Let's remember that according to the policy paper-making average beds in our country it is about 74% So you could say that 1/4 of the beds are empty, the potential is huge. Therefore, why not develop this potential? If operating rooms, which cost a lot, tens, hundreds of millions on a national scale, work until hours. 14, 15, then why couldn't these halls work this afternoon on weekends if someone wanted to buy a benefit there? The public healthcare provider, who could offer such benefits, should organise the provision of benefits in such a way that there is no restriction on access to the health benefits covered by the contract with the National Health Fund or the change in the conditions for its implementation, i.e. it must, as I said, ensure the separation of staff, equipment, premises necessary for the implementation of benefits and sources of funding, and prevent the rights of persons on waiting lists from being violated. The NFZ, as a party to a public-funded health care contract under the above-mentioned law, may review the performance of the contract and, in particular, verify that patients are not charged undue fees. The collection of undue charges shall be governed by Article. Paragraph 193 (4) of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare and § Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point (1) (a) c Annex to the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 8 September 2015 on the general terms and conditions of health care contracts. According to Art. 193 point 4 of the Act on public-funded healthcare provision: who collects an undue charge on insured persons for benefits covered by a contract with a healthcare fund, is fined, i.e. the sanctioning of irregular practices in the current legal order is very strong. The supervision of possible inappropriate practices on the part of SPOZs, which would like to force patients to buy commercial benefits, is described very closely. It may be subject to very strict control. So here we don't see the danger of creating, I don't know, the pressure on patients to choose benefits paid instead of benefits to which they are authorised as part of insurance with the National Health Fund. As regards the doubts as to the compatibility of the proposed provisions with the Constitution, we would like to inform you that there are two paragraphs of the Constitution on the compatibility of the charging of patients for health services: the provisions of the Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2. According to the first one, everyone has the right to health protection, but it is such a generally formulated provision that it indicates that every person is the subject of this right. [[♪ The bell ♪]] This provision should be read in the context of Article. 30, i.e. the right to health protection concerns fundamental rights, i.e. dignity. In view of the above, each person should have the right to take any action within the limits of the law that allows him to exercise those powers. So the right of the patient is that he will be able to buy benefits with his money if he has to. This is the exercise of the right of the patient, not the imposition of the need to buy commercial benefits for patients. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u60	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen! To answer this question, I would like to remind and inform you that the pre-recognized groups of fruit and vegetables producers received a total of over PLN 7.6 billion of EU and national funds. No agricultural production sector has ever received such support. Some of these groups have set up well-functioning companies that manage the market, which actually exploit the accumulated potential. Unfortunately, some of the groups, whether because of their inability to manage, or because of their deliberate action, used the money in an improper way. Following the audits carried out in these groups by the Polish institutions, as well as the audits carried out by the auditors of the European Commission and the European Court of Auditors, there were allegations that there were situations where groups were created in an apparent way only to obtain grants. In addition, there have been cases where the group members themselves sold their own assets at excessive prices to the group. There have been cases of over-investment costs and purchases. There have been other irregularities which have unfortunately been confirmed in the case of a large part of the groups. 209 groups were included in the corrective action plan. As part of the corrective action plan, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture verified the correctness of 1107 aid applications for 193 groups and it was established that 43% those applications contain irregularities. These were irregularities of various kinds and the various sanctions involved. These irregularities were sometimes very small and sometimes significant. For 25% Applications for recovery of funds unduly collected have been initiated. 279 decisions have been taken on the funds unduly paid. In the case of 27 groups, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture requested the head of the National Tax Administration to carry out a check on the regularity of the spending of funds originating in the European Union and also, in the case of 27 groups, sent a notice to the prosecutor's office of suspicion of the offence. It appears from the information available that the national prosecutor has taken personal supervision of ongoing proceedings. The final clearance of what has been done with regard to the expenditure of these funds will be possible after the resolution plan has been completed and the administrative procedure has been exhausted, as a significant proportion of the proceedings are currently in the review process until the second instance or before the administrative courts. Currently, 267 fruit and vegetable producer organisations are in the register kept by the Agricultural Market Agency and 14 groups are still implementing the investigation plan to be recognised. The data from the register does not confirm that claims for bankruptcy have already been made, which does not mean that there are no signals about the difficult situation of some of the groups resulting from very different reasons. Unfortunately, when State aid or EU aid it has already been granted to the organisation concerned, it is not possible to grant the aid again and the Ministry and the Polish State cannot therefore provide assistance to these groups, these producer organisations. The mechanism mentioned by your Member may be applied, but without public funding. Public funds cannot be used for this purpose, so there would have to be an economic institution that would have undertaken such activities on a commercial basis, on a market basis. So, in conclusion, I would like to say that the situation of the producer groups is very diverse. In April 2016, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation has drawn up a document on the development plan for inland waterways in Poland for the years 2016 and 2020 with a view to 2030, which was adopted on 14 June 2016 by the Council of Ministers. This document sets out directions for the development of inland waterway transport in Poland. The consequences of the measures adopted by the Ministry were the ratification by President Andrzej Duda on 6 March 2017 of the AGN Agreement, namely the European Agreement on major inland waterways of international importance. Poland, as a member of the AGN Agreement, undertakes to ensure on the waterways covered by the Agreement navigation conditions meeting the criteria applicable to inland waterways of international importance in the so-called. Class E, corresponding to at least class IV sailing. The infrastructure of inland waterways is multi-purpose. In addition to transport, flood prevention, water security for citizens, agriculture and industry and water energy, it is also important to use rivers for water tourism and recreation. The Ministry's plans include: to achieve significant improvements in navigation conditions on the Vistula Water River and its inclusion in the European water network. It is initially estimated that the cost of the Vistula cascade in the section of Warsaw - Gdańsk will be PLN 31.5 billion. The Ministry begins work on the preparation of feasibility studies for the Odrzańska Water Road E30 and the Water Road of the River Wisła E40 Warsaw - Gdańsk. The next step will be to order an analysis of the demand for transport services on the Odrzaska Waterway and the Water River of Vistula. The analysis will answer questions about the interest of @AKAPIT = companies providing inland navigation services. Its main objective will be to forecast the development of the inland waterway transport sector, defining the size, structure and direction of transport carried. From the information obtained from the Minister of the Environment, it appears that the water level construction project in Niepołomice on the upper Vistula has been included in the list of inland water projects, is currently at 25th position. Due to the limited allocation of resources for inland waterway projects under the Operational Programme, the infrastructure and the environment will not cover all investments. In short, given the degree of preparation of this project, it will not be possible to implement it in this perspective. However, in the assumptions for the development plan for inland waterways in Poland for the years 2016 and 2020 with a view to 2030, the construction of the water level in Niepołomice was placed as an investment expected to be carried out in the long term. Pay and other things are in the hands of government, old people, that's deducted from taxes, that's normal employment. The wages at work offices are very low, on an embarrassing level, so to speak, and that is also a big problem when it comes to work offices. We have introduced some methods to improve the efficiency and co-financing of the Labour Fund activities more active, i.e. customer advisors, there is this special supplement, 5% , and efficiency - 2%. This instrument is in force until the end of 2017 and we need to extend it for the following years because it also requires that these funds be earmarked for this. Now when it comes to checking whether it's an efficiency test. Of course, I have the data on cost efficiency in various forms of support. For the trainings that you spoke about, 5500 PLN on average. Whether it's much or little, it depends on the situation. In one case it will be too much, in the other it will not be enough. That's the question of how we're gonna approach it. Intervention work - 6600 on average, public works - 12 300 (roads, public works). It's an expensive instrument most often used by local governments. But we also need to consider who uses these instruments, what people use them. If we are talking about the most excluded group, i.e. those who are affected by the highest unemployment rate, then of course there is a different situation. This is very important, and it must also be stressed that if we look at the structure of unemployment, we are talking about very low unemployment at 5.5 on a national scale, but we still have very diverse unemployment. An example of Warsaw: the centre of Warsaw is less than 2% And the Szydłowiec district is over 20%, that's 10 times the difference. That's a whole different way to look at it. in a county like this, or in a big city. It is similar across the country that unemployment is very diverse, and the actions we will take - but also to change what I will talk about later - in the new labour market law, they must take into account this condition of differentiation, so that these funds are used to a greater extent. Returning to cost efficiency, internships - very popular, form of support for people, where after the internship period you can take up work: 8900 PLN average. As I say, there is also a discussion: much, little - depending on the situation. She can be large if an internship is given in the city of Warsaw, but it can be small when it comes to an internship, which will be e.g. in the Szydłowiec district. One-off start-up measures: more than 20 000 zł. I answered Mrs Boris-Raccoon's questions just a moment ago. A very good instrument, which is very successful. The unemployed person can start an economic activity, receives a subsidy of 20,000, up to six times, here we have an average for last year 20 600 PLN. This activity needs to be carried out for 12 months, and later this efficiency is included in the further part of the employment over the next 3 months. It is worth considering whether this instrument should not be extended, how to use it even more, to create sustainable jobs, so that these measures are not actually used only, as you say in your question, for short-term power supply. I'm talking about starting a business. Refund of the costs of fitting-out or fitting-out of a workstation used by entrepreneurs employing workers under similar conditions: also a similar amount of PLN 20 800. If we look at all the performance indicators we're looking at, they're 11,000. zł. And now... I'm looking at the time I'm running out. Reform and new law in 2014. One of the topics that we discussed hard at the time, when working in the subcommittee, was that we did not agree to some solutions, because there was a question, for example. profiling - that is what the Member spoke about in her speech. I was not in favour of profiling and I am not, because I believe that it stiffens the work of labour offices, that people are sorted out, assigns to particular groups, or looks for on the Internet, to which profile to save to support such support, so in the new proposals we will want to introduce this as an optional, non-mandatory task. If one of the labour offices thinks he needs it, he'll benefit if he doesn't, it won't apply. On issues related to the loan system, there has also been a discussion over many years: grants - loans. We chose the measure: we have the subsidies, and the loans. I think this is working today because one-off grants are a good instrument, but loans are also a good instrument for this reason, that if someone takes a loan and has to pay it back, he will think more about what activity to run it on. It is known that these are most often services, this is trade, because this is the easiest when it comes to starting up a business. And that's an instrument that's worth talking about. Of course, we are preparing to change the law or write a new law, simpler, bigger in which we want more flexibility in the functioning of labour offices. In public communication, there was a proposal that I made to return the labour offices to the government administration. There's a lot of craziness going on around here, but we're working on it. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u183	Dear Marshal! Undoubtedly, amendments to this draft law are necessary. The continuous increase in the number of offences affecting the life and health of animals, including those in a qualified form, is an undeniable basis for concluding that the current criminal legislation does not fulfil its function. The draft is a response to existing problems, primarily introducing increased penalties for killing animals and animal abuse, as well as a mandatory judgment on the purpose of social organisations' activities, on the objective of protecting animals when convicted of an offence against animal life and animal health. In order to achieve the effectiveness of the bans in question, the draft law introduces very much needed regulations on the ways in which courts and other institutions, in particular at local level, comply with those prohibitions by offenders throughout the period for which they have been ordered. High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club supports the draft law and the four amendments tabled and adopted at the joint meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. These amendments introduce changes which aim to clarify the rules by limiting their scope. It is considered that live fish intended for sale should only be kept in an aquatic environment, as opposed to the requirement to keep fish for other purposes, e.g. as part of a farm in which it is permitted, in accordance with professional guidelines, to temporarily keep fish without water, of course, provided that they are able to breathe. Amendments concerning Article. 35 Acts are also intended to mitigate the prohibition on all activities and occupations and any activities related to the use or impact of animals. The Law and Justice Club also fully agrees with these amendments. The Law and Justice Club supports this government bill, together with the amendments. Thank you very much.

Abuse is, on the other hand, any of the ones mentioned in Article. 2 (2) 2 Laws on how to deal directly with an animal which must be targeted directly by the perpetrator. Thus, the intention refers to the act itself, not to causing suffering or pain. Today, prosecutors very often refer to this part of the justification of the Supreme Court by dying to investigate abuse. David Karaś, author of the report "As Poles abuse animals," emphasizes that the authorities applying the law do not take into account the suffering of animals, which is a side effect of non-targeted man's actions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u238	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to say that the draft law submitted by the Polish Sejm on the amendment of the law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and some other laws is a very important regulation for the efficient and efficient use of EU funds. This document was drawn up on the basis of a previous audit on the effectiveness of the implementation of the operational programmes of the financial perspective 2014-2020, which resulted in the identification of barriers and restrictions to the use of Union funds. I will remind you that when this audit was carried out at the end of 2015, our country, Poland, in terms of the use of European funds from this financial perspective 2014 In order to implement corrective actions, the Minister for Development and Finance has developed a special action plan to increase efficiency and speed up the implementation of the Partnership Agreement 2014-2020 programmes, which was adopted by the Council of Ministers in February 2016. The bill that was prepared was the result of these actions. The solutions contained in the bill submitted to the High House are the result of a broad discussion with the social partners, local authorities, institutions for the implementation of EU funds, as well as beneficiaries of funds from the European Union over several months. The proposed amendment is therefore the result of almost 2 years of experience in implementing the operational programmes of cohesion policy in this financial perspective. The essence of the solutions contained in the draft law is the introduction of improvements and simplifications in the system of implementation of EU funds, which is so important that the proposed law has been incorporated into the package so-called. the business constitution, i.e. proposals for legal solutions that involve the reconstruction of economic law and the introduction of facilitations primarily for entrepreneurs. The aim of the proposed amendments is both to simplify the implementation of projects funded by beneficiaries and to improve the implementation of operational programmes by the various institutions. The bill contains a number of simplifications, facilitations, and maybe I will mention only some of them. It will therefore be easier for those applying for Union funds to prohibit the competent institutions from requiring certificates and documents from those bodies to which the institution will have access. This applies e.g. data from KRS, ZUS, tax offices. In order to speed up the evaluation of projects in the appeal procedure, the deadlines for examining the protests were significantly shortened and the possibility for the applicant to withdraw the protest was introduced. In the amendment we introduce a new function: the ombudsman of European funds, who will act in favour of beneficiaries, takes care of the issues of all those interested in using the funds. It will also be responsible for the collection of proposals and requests for operational programmes to improve the system. As part of the implementation of the projects, beneficiaries will no longer be subject to penalties for payment of interest if they are late in submitting a payment application by 14 days. Notwithstanding these changes, the draft law will limit the number of documents - programming guidelines, which beneficiaries will apply under the signed grant agreement. Right now we have the so-called. horizontal guidelines and programme-specific guidelines. We will no longer use these guidelines for each operational programme. These are changes to the beneficiaries. We also introduce other facilitations, e.g. the possibility of changing partners in the partner project with the agreement of the competent institution. It's been virtually impossible so far. In addition, in order to better implement operational programmes, voivodships will be involved in the implementation of operational programmes as representatives of the Minister responsible for regional development carrying out the tasks of the Member State. They will participate in the designation process and participate as observers in the work of the monitoring committees and the evaluation committees of projects under the regional operational programmes. High Seym! I would like to assure you that the proposed amendment to the Act on the principles for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and 2020 will improve and make use of EU funds more efficient and thus contribute to the faster economic and social development of our country. In view of the above, I would ask you to continue to proceed with the bill by the High House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u240	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The aim of the draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and 2020 and some other laws is to introduce improvements and simplifications and to ensure the efficient use of the funds of the European Union. The proposed provisions are a consequence of the proposals and requests made by the institutions and bodies involved in the implementation and spending of funds in the 2014-2020 financial perspective, and meet them. The most important regulations simplifying the application for measures and facilitating the implementation of projects include, in particular: limiting the number of documents necessary for the effective submission of the application for funding and the conclusion of the grant agreement; separating the criteria for selecting projects from the formal conditions which may be supplemented by the applicant at the request of the competent institution; setting up an institution for the Ombudsman of the European Funds; the possibility of requesting the applicant to correct the proposal's application for funding under the selection criteria laid down in the rules of the competition; dividing the competition into rounds; changes in the rules for implementing the partnership projects; the possibility of withdrawing the protest; shortening the time limits for processing the remedies; making changes in the functioning of the registers available, i.e. a register of entities excluded by the possibility of electronic communication within the existing registers and a register of entities that ensure the proper provision of development services; introducing a 14-day transfer of interest period in case the beneficiary fails to submit an application for payment within a time limit or for an appropriate amount; enabling the payment of special subsidies granted to beneficiaries from the State budget and the budgets of local government units to co-finance projects on an equivalent basis as for payments from the European budget, i.e. within the deadlines resulting from the co-financing agreement, without the need to reimburse unused funds at the end of the budget period; introducing the possibility of creating regional development funds; introducing regulations enabling greater cooperation between entrepreneurs, universities and educational entities through the creation and functioning of competence councils - this will enable the economic staff to adapt to the current needs of entrepreneurs operating on the labour market; taking into account the participation of voivodships in the implementation of European Union funds in the financial perspective of 2014.2020. High Izbo! According to the Parliamentary Club, the Law and Justice Act will be a good solution for a better, efficient, flexible and efficient use of European funds. The project offers the opportunity for many actors, entrepreneurs, universities, local governments to focus primarily on development and not on the implementation of cumbersome procedures. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the proposed government project expected by the beneficiaries and is in favour of directing it to further work. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u241	Thank you. Mr Janusz Cichoń, Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u242	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the opinion of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 I would like to say first of all that some of the solutions put in place really fit well in the objectives of the amendment and meet the needs of potential applicants and beneficiaries of European Union funds, but unfortunately not all. I will focus on those who raise our serious doubts. Firstly, such doubts are raised by provisions which go beyond the objective of the amendment, as well as those which entail only apparent simplifications. The inclusion of the voivodeship in the system of implementation of regional operational programmes deserves particular attention here, which, incidentally, took place at the last stage of the work on the amendment. This raises resistance in the light of the fact that it is the voivodship boards that act as the managing authorities of regional operational programmes, and thus bear full responsibility for the lawful and effective implementation of this task. We do not see the point of including an additional representative of the government in this system, nor do we see any benefits that would result from this, especially since, at the same time, a large representation of the government party will take part in the work of the Committees on the basis of this law and the guidelines of the Minister responsible for Development. It is also worth remembering that the voivodship in the framework of supervision examines compliance with the law of resolutions of the management board of the voivodship. Among these resolutions are also resolutions determining recruitments. In this respect, we believe that these proposed rules do not add any added value to the project selection process, that they actually duplicate the mechanisms already in place in the system to ensure the proper representation of the parties, and that they artificially extend the voivodeship tasks and, most importantly, undermine the independence of local authorities in the management of regional operational programmes. The draft also includes regulations on the use of funds for the implementation of financial instruments. And here is another issue that raises our serious doubts, because it has been decided that the funds earmarked for the implementation of these financial instruments, returned by final recipients, are not the income of the authorising officer or any other entity to whom the authorising officer could entrust the implementation of financial instruments or feedback, because that is also the case. On the one hand, we are not really dealing with the problem of determining who owns these measures, and on the other, we are going beyond the objective, because the means returning from these instruments no longer constitute the resources of the operational programmes, so they are in fact not EU funds and the way in which they are implemented should not be regulated by this law. In our view, the rules unduly limit the use of funds by the competent institutions, i.e. the voivodship boards, for this purpose. This is clearly a reduction in the independence of local governments in the conduct of development policy. Another issue that we are resisting is the establishment of a new institution for the Ombudsman of European funds. This is replicating to a large extent the tasks that are being carried out today, and this will lead to the extension of existing structures, and we do not see any qualitative benefits resulting from this fact. We are very concerned about what you are doing when it comes to territorial contracts, because in fact what you are proposing is giving up this tool. As a matter of fact, [[♪ The bell ♪]] We are losing the concept of a territorial contract as such, as a mechanism for coordinating development policy in voivodships. We have a number of other comments. However, we believe that this project needs to be dealt with and that these shortcomings will be removed. [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u272	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you so much for your interest and for all those words. I would also like to complete my earlier statement and answer the questions of ladies and gentlemen. Firstly, I would like to say once again that the issue of this law was the introduction of the greatest possible facilitations, simplifications and, above all, for the beneficiaries of these funds, i.e. even less for the institutions and much more for the beneficiaries. I am referring here to both entrepreneurs and local governments and NGOs. I'll remind you again what was driving us. In November 2015, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) Poland had certified, i.e. sent and confirmed, funds to the European Commission of exactly zero euros or zero zlotys. We have not provided any funds, we have been in the last place in the European Union. Of course, we had an earlier perspective, but this condition was unacceptable to us. Secondly, it was important to launch this perspective as soon as possible, hence the audit I mentioned, hence the special plan that we have prepared. On the basis of this, too, we have already introduced a lot of solutions that were procedural or organisational solutions that did not require legislative changes. What we decided to do... We have also decided to introduce the legislative changes that we are presenting here, solutions that needed to be amended, especially in the framework of the implementation law. I can already say to you that many of these organisational arrangements have had such a positive effect that today we are clearly a leader in the use of European funds. We never had such a good use of funds - relatively, in relation to other countries - we had. At the moment, the funds paid by the European Commission to Poland 34% All the resources. Let me remind you that Poland has a stake of 22.6. On average, in previous years it was at 15%, that's half of it. So it can be said that cohesion policy is very strongly based on Poland. European Commission every time it repeats, just like yesterday, when we had the so-called. a high-level meeting on railway projects. The Commission strongly confirms this. I will refer to concrete solutions. I must say, referring to Mr Owl's words, that I too have considerable experience in the implementation of projects. The first project I carried out in 1991, so I have 26 years of experience in implementing projects from EU funds. As far as the solutions we are proposing here are concerned, I should like to start with the Ombudsman of European funds. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very strong democratic solution, which is opened up by the institutions in various areas to the beneficiaries of the solution. We also use them: and in the public sector, like the Ombudsman, and in the private sector, for example. a spokesperson in financial institutions. We don't see any reason why this solution should be introduced here. However, I must say that as far as this solution is concerned, the prototype was the spokesman appointed by the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development. At the moment this solution, this new function will be mandatory for all managing authorities, such as regions whether we as a ministry, for individual programmes and will be optional for intermediate institutions. We know a lot of them want to use it. We, as responsible for European funds, simply want to be the front of the beneficiaries and listen to them. We believe that this is a solution that will undoubtedly help us to do so. The second thing I wanted to say was financial instruments, feedback instruments. Because our assumption is this: the feedback instruments are to work for the development of our country, our regions over the years, and not just in a given financial perspective. Here I may refer to the attempt by the Minister of Finance in the previous government, who tried this money, as it is said, to absorb into the budget, which would mean that it might reduce the budget deficit, but unfortunately would not work for the development of our country. This solution will allow for the multiple use of these feedback funds for the development of our regions. The next issue is territorial contracts. Here one of your fellow Members referred to what we propose as a dummy. The problem is, these contracts are a fake right now. Why? Because many projects have been included in them, for which there is absolutely no funding. Therefore, they have unfortunately become a somewhat theoretical tool. We want to reinstate this instrument and set an example. At the moment, we are conducting an annexation of these contracts so that they can finance cultural and national heritage projects in all regions that want them to do so. Besides, the answer is very positive. All regions have responded to this, so it is clear that the practical approach is certainly well assessed here. The role of the voivodship. Well, please note that in the previous financial perspective for the period 2007-2013, the role of the voivode was much stronger. It was the voivode who was responsible for certifying all expenses, which means he confirmed all expenses, so it is not true that there is no potential in voivodship offices to carry out tasks... [[Not anymore.]] ...related to European funds. What we care about right now is the quality of the projects and we want the voivodships to help us not as active participants in this process, just to help us with surveillance. At the moment, in 13 regions, i.e. 13 regional committees, the voivodships have their representatives who have the right to speak. In fact, we're changing their role. We want them to be in all regions, but as observers, not as active as they are now in 13 regions, but as observers. The second change that we really care about is the participation of the voivodships, but once again as observers in the project evaluation committees. Why? Please read the local newspapers, how many complaints there are about the selection of projects. This is - I'm telling you right now - our biggest flaw, i.e. the choice of good projects, and the role of the voivode is to be limited to an observer. He won't participate in the election process, he's only here as an observer. We hope that this will improve the use of resources. Ladies and gentlemen, have we used other votes in preparation? Absolutely. I set up a simplification team, which works very regularly, meets, is designed just so that the practitioners can tell us what we need to change. These changes are intended to ensure that, as one Member has rightly pointed out here, neither one euro is lost from our money. We must take care of every euro that we receive here absolutely. The question is, why are we turning the voivodships on? Also because we want this system to work more efficiently. This is a decentralised system that we support, which is implemented by local governments, while the government is responsible for the whole implementation of the policy and we want the voivodships to help us do so. Thank you very much, Marshal, High Izbo. The construction of the Świnna Poręba tank has lasted for over 30 years, i.e. Since 1986, the Law of 4 March 2005 on the establishment of a multi-annual programme under the name of the "Programme for the construction of the Swine Poręba Water Tank in the years 2006-2010' was intended to ensure that this construction was completed by 2010. Unfortunately, the provisions of this law concerning the timetable for the execution of works have not been implemented. Date extended by another 2 years It wasn't kept either. Moreover, the continuity of financing of this important investment was lost for Kraków and Małopolska. R., allows for a peaceful completion of this construction on the basis of funding of 53 230 thousand. zł for the necessary work to be carried out. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u300	The position of the PSL Club on the information of the Minister of the Environment on the implementation in 2016 of the tasks set out in the Act of 13 May 2016 on the completion of the construction of the Swine Poręba Water Tank. The construction of the Świnna Poręba reservoir is probably one of the longest ongoing hydrotechnical projects in Poland. The first project was created in the 1950s, and the construction started in 1986. In 2005, the Sejm adopted a timetable for completing the investment, but in the context of the financial crisis the construction of the vessel was stopped in 2009. However, in 2010 the government decided to finalise this investment. The flood of the tank was planned for 2015. Until then, approximately PLN 2.5 billion had been spent. This year the Sejm adopted a law assuming that this year over PLN 46.53 million will be built and PLN 6.7 million in the next year (the Act supported PSL). Most of the money is spent on stabilizing landslips - 25,7 million PLN in 2 years. The reconstruction of roads will cost PLN 20.6 million. The remaining PLN 6.93 million will be allocated to investments related to the vessel, which include among others. construction of dams, tanks and power plants, demolition of asphalt roads and sealing of the control and injection gallery during water flooring. The money will come from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, and the completion of the construction will rest on the shoulders of the Regional Water Management Board in Krakow. As a PSL club, we will support activities aimed at completing the investment, which is expected in particular by the inhabitants of Małopolska, who can also gain a recreational area generating new jobs, because Świnna Poręba will be the largest water in the region, which will visit more than a dozen million tourists each year.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u303	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! I am very pleased to present on behalf of the Commission for European Union Affairs a report by the Commission on the Commission's draft resolution on the recognition of the draft directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the work-life balance of parents and carers and repealing Council Directive 2010/18/EU as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Commission for European Union Affairs at its 134th meeting on 7 June in accordance with Article. Paragraph 7 4 of the Act of 8 October 2010 on cooperation between the Council of Ministers and the Sejm and the Senate on matters relating to the membership of the Republic of Poland in the European Union has examined the draft Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the balance between working life and private parents and carers and repealing Council Directive 2010/18/EU - COM (2017) 253 and the draft position of the Government relating to it. The Commission adopted opinion 21 which does not support the draft directive and considers it incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity, considering that the European Commission has not sufficiently justified that the objectives pursued by the proposed directive cannot be better achieved by regulation at national level. At its 138th meeting, the European Union Commission adopted a draft resolution on the recognition of the draft directive on work-life balance of parents and carers as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. This project was immediately submitted to the marshal's staff. On 8 June 2017, the Marshal sent a letter in accordance with Article. 148cc (paragraph 148c) 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm draft resolution to the European Union Committee for First Reading. At its 139th meeting on 20 June 2017, the Commission carried out a first reading of the Commission's draft resolution on the recognition of the draft Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the work-life balance of parents and carers and repealing Council Directive 2010/18/EU as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity, printing No 1628. The Commission, having considered this draft resolution at its meeting on 20 June 2017, adopted a report, in print No 1644 requesting the adoption of a draft resolution without amendments. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'd like to say a few words of justification. The proposed Directive is intended to significantly affect the balance between working life and private life of working parents and carers while repealing the current Directive 2010/18/EU. The aim of the project seems justified and desirable, given in particular the intended strengthening of the role of the family in upbringing, which would be more effective through, for example, more detailed arrangements for leave, flexibility of organisation, working time or increased participation of men in the use of these forms of work organisation, which would give them equal opportunities for women to take more care of their children and raise them. However, according to the Sejm, regulation at European Union level must take into account the arrangements in force in Member States' legislation. EU regulation should not interfere with national legal solutions which effectively ensure the proper level of protection of the family in the area of child care and education. The Sejm recognises that, despite the legitimacy of the EU legislator's objective of strengthening the role of the family, both parents in the care and education of the offspring, the draft directive violates the principle of subsidiarity because the European Commission has not sufficiently justified that the objectives of the European Union to be pursued by the proposed directive can be better achieved at Union level. According to Art. 5 Protocol No 2 on the application of the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, annexed to the Treaty on European Union and to the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, should contain a detailed statement enabling the assessment of compliance with the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. Such a statement should include data enabling the financial impact assessment of the draft act concerned and, in the case of the Directive, its impact on the regulations introduced by the Member States, including, where appropriate, regional legislation. The conclusion that the Union objective can be better achieved at Union level is justified on the basis of qualitative indicators and, where possible, quantitative indicators. Draft legislative acts shall take into account the need to minimise any financial or administrative burden on the Union, national governments, regional or local authorities, economic operators and citizens and to ensure that such burdens are commensurate with the intended objective. The justification given by the European Commission for the draft directive does not refer to Polish legislation which ensures a high level of protection of the family in the field of child care and education. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above, I would like to ask you kindly to support the draft which is currently justified by the opinion in which the Sejm states that the draft directive does not comply with the principle of subsidiarity. I would like to stress that this proposal violates this principle because the European Commission has not sufficiently justified that the objectives of the European Union, which is to be pursued by the proposed directive, can be better achieved at European Union level. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u307	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! This directive aims to better harmonise professional life, professional responsibilities and family responsibilities. It is, like other directives, the introduction of certain minimum standards throughout the European Union, which all countries would be required to implement within 2 years. It concerns maternity, paternity, caring and parental leave, and concerns the flexibility of work. In all these cases there are Polish regulations, often quite advanced and often going further than this directive, but in some cases our legislation will require some additions or remodeling. I believe that for the benefit of this legislation and for the benefit of those with young children. First, maybe I should start with whether this directive is based on the European Treaties. It is based on the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 1, which states that - I quote: the Union supports and complements the activities of the Member States in the following areas - and this is mentioned here - equality between men and women with regard to their employability and treatment at work. If we say that we already have many of these things used, let's say this: e.g. There are very many environmental rules in Sweden at a very high level, but if the European Union also introduces them to other countries where they are not applied at such a level, that does not mean that this is contrary to the principle of subsidiarity. The Union wants to apply a minimum level to all countries. And that is also the intention of this project to have this minimum level in all countries. In Poland, in some cases this level is quite clearly exceeded, and in others it will require some addition. Where are the most important points that require some changes in Polish legislation? They relate primarily to parental leave. In Poland we now have after paternity leave, after maternity leave 32 weeks parental leave paid for the first 6 weeks 100% , then - 60% which can be used by a father or a mother, can be given this holiday as if to pass. The directive introduces something like this: 4 months of this leave will only be for a specific parent - 4 months will have a father and 4 months will have a mother, and they cannot give up on it. So either we need to remodel this solution, or we need to add additional powers, which would be much more expensive for a father to have that right, but without giving up on his mother. Secondly, the directive extends the use of this leave from 6 years to 12 years, so some additional costs that would be here, perhaps would be spread over more years, so that at the moment these costs would not be particularly higher. However, this is very important because in Poland this parental leave is very little used by fathers and as many as 90% of women complain that they are not treated equally on the labour market, among others. because of that. Also when it comes to childhood, yes, we care about childhood, more births. One of the main causes and barriers to increasing the number of children and deciding on motherhood is the difficulty of combining family responsibilities with professional responsibilities. The implementation of this Directive would facilitate this. Another example of this directive is flexible working time. We have flexible working time, work at home, part-time work and teleworking everywhere. These solutions exist in our Labour Code, but firstly, they can probably be extended, and secondly, the directive gives additional powers to parents with children up to 12 years of age. Namely, they may submit such an application to the employer and if the employer refuses to grant him flexible working time, he must justify it. So this strengthens the right to flexible working time. In total, I believe that this project will strengthen equality of treatment in the labour market in Poland of women, mothers and fathers, improve childhood, will not cost too much and will also contribute to the fact that in Europe we will have raised minimum standards. So I think we'd make a mistake if we rejected him. [[♪ The bell ♪]] He should be supported. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u311	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Poselski Club I present the club's position on the draft resolution on the recognition of the draft directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the balance between working life and private parents and carers and repealing Council Directive 2010/18/EU as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity, print No 1628, together with the committee's report, print No 1644. In the explanatory memorandum of the draft resolution, the applicant does not question the validity and normative content of the Directive of the European Council, but only questions its adoption at EU level, questioning precisely the application of the Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Treaty on European Union, i.e. It is the principle of subsidiarity. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u323	We will proceed to the vote on the committee's resolution in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u324	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to present a draft law on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act, which we used to call the Act on accelerated depreciation. The main objective of the proposed change is to create a mechanism to stimulate investments made by entrepreneurs, including, we assume, and this is presented in the impact assessment of regulation, especially by small and medium-sized enterprises. The proposed instrument, the proposed legal solution, will provide the possibility of annual single settlement of fixed capital expenditure up to 100,000. zł, with a minimum value of 10 thousand. zł. We are talking about the possibility for entrepreneurs to accumulate such expenses, provided that they are not less than 10 000. PLN and does not exceed 100,000. £ per year. There is also an expansion of the circle of players who will be able to benefit from this settlement - as it will be an instrument not covered by the de minimis regime, it can also be used by entrepreneurs who have benefited in recent years, for example. with the funding of various programmes from the European Union. We hope that the possibility to make such a one-off depreciation will encourage entrepreneurs - we already have such signals - to invest in machines and devices, because these are also categories indicated in the catalogue of those that can be counted down. At the same time, it is an instrument that appears on the market at a time when European measures are practically preventing entrepreneurs from expanding the machinery park, because such measures simply do not exist in this financial perspective. So, seeing this gap in the market, we hope that this instrument will make such investments feasible for small and medium-sized enterprises. In addition, this law solves a long-standing problem concerning entrepreneurs, legal persons and organisational units without legal personality, which e.g. buy tickets from foreign air carriers. The proposed amendment aims at abolishing the current obligation of Polish economic operators 10% tax on revenues from air tickets, because in practice it is impossible or significantly impeded to carry out this obligation. What does it look like, what is the scope of the proposed changes? According to the draft article. Article 22 (2) (b) 14 and 15 Natural Persons Income Tax Act and Art. 16 The Corporate Income Tax Act provides for the introduction of a single settlement mechanism for investment expenditure up to 100 000. PLN per year by including them in the cost of obtaining revenue as a result of write-downs made. The proposed deductions will apply to fixed assets of groups 3 and 6 and 8 of the classification of fixed assets, which is precisely the machinery and equipment I mentioned. Real estate - buildings and constructions - and means of transport were excluded from the scope of this solution. These regulations will allow taxable persons purchasing new fixed assets which have not been used before either by that buyer or by another taxable person. This acquisition may take place both in ownership and in joint ownership, in accordance with the general arrangements in force in income taxes. The new rules will allow taxpayers of PIT and CIT, whose investments in fixed assets in a given year amounted to at least 10 000. PLN and related to new fixed assets. The limit mentioned is 100,000. PLN includes the total amount of depreciation and the amount of payment made for the delivery of the fixed asset. In addition, the project foresees that the taxpayer will be able to include 10 000 in the limit. zloty value of several fixed assets, each of these fixed assets must be higher than 3,5 thousand. zł. So, as I said at the beginning, we are convinced that this is primarily a tool for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs, for whom this expense, as we say, is up to 100,000. PLN is an important expenditure in the company's budget. At the same time, if the taxpayer starts depreciation of the asset - and this is also an important change - with a value of less than 10 000. PLN and over 3.5 thousand. zł and then take into account its value in the limit of 10,000. zł, then the depreciation write-downs made so far remain in the cost and include a single write-down of 100,000. the value of the fixed asset is subject to the unimpreciated value of the asset. Where the taxpayer incurs an investment expense of more than 100,000. zł, this makes a single write-down to that amount in the tax year and the remaining surplus will be able to absorb according to the general rules in the following tax years, the sum of the write-downs and payments referred to in Article. 22, which is included in the cost of obtaining revenue, must not exceed the initial value of those fixed assets. Taxpayers will be able to benefit from the proposed solutions regardless of their form of business, as I mentioned earlier. However, in the case of operating in the form of a company which is not a legal person, the depreciation limit of 100,000. will concern all the partners of this company. Given that the current rules provide a solution for a single depreciation of up to 50,000. The euro - addressed to small taxpayers and start-ups - is in the case of acquisition of a fixed measure giving entitlement to a single write-off of both 50 000. The euro and the proposed amount of 100,000. PLN write-off on this asset will only be able to be made by the taxpayer on the basis of one of these arrangements. It's obvious, of course, that the bill provides for a one-off deduction. In addition, it is proposed in Article. 22 enabling taxable persons to count directly in the cost of obtaining the revenue of payments made, e.g. advances on the acquisition of a fixed asset the delivery of which will be carried out in subsequent reporting periods, i.e. next month, quarter or year. It is also such a preference for small and medium-sized enterprises, for which the acquisition of fixed assets is often spread over time, the accumulation of funds for a given expenditure also postpones the acquisition of a fixed asset itself. Taking into account the advance payment made will allow taxable persons who purchase such a permanent measure to be included in the costs of obtaining the investment effort in advance. At the same time, this advance will then be taken into account by reducing the amount of a single write-down. These solutions are dictated by the fact that the advance relates to a fixed asset and is included in its initial value, i.e. in the expenditure on its acquisition and therefore in the limit of a single depreciation write-off. At the same time, the principle of determining the income from the paid disposal of the fixed asset to which the advance relates was maintained when proposing these arrangements. The revenue from the paid disposal of such a fixed asset will be determined by reducing revenue by an initial value taking into account the depreciation write-offs made from that fixed asset. The advance paid, although included in the initial value of the fixed asset, does not reduce this value in determining the proceeds from the disposal of the fixed asset. The advance is also not a depreciation copy. For this reason, the difference between the revenue and the initial value of the fixed asset in determining the income from the sale of the fixed asset will not be increased by the advance paid on the fixed asset. How do we estimate the impact of this regulation? As I said, first of all, we estimate that this will be a law that will mainly concern small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. In 2015, the tax due in companies showed more than 175 thousand. CIT taxpayers, more than 400 thousand. PIT-36 and 727 000 payers PIT-36L payers. At the same time, this is the number of entities that will be able to benefit from the proposed one-off settlement of investment expenditure. Of course, we know that not all bets, not all of them will benefit from this possibility or even part of it, because not all entrepreneurs will invest more than 10,000. annually or will not be cost-effective due to the business profile. On the basis of the current business structure and calculations we have carried out at the Ministry of Development, we assume that about 154 000 can benefit from the programme. entrepreneurs, therefore, we assume that this is a significant circle of beneficiaries of this legal solution. In countries belonging to the European Union, the system of income deductions is, of course, an established practice to stimulate an increase in investment expenditure. In the UK it is possible to deduct one-off depreciation of fixed assets excluding passenger cars and therefore a similar solution as in our case, with a value not higher than 200,000. Pounds. Similar solutions are in Spain, and Hungary has introduced the possibility of one-off depreciation of the advance on the purchase of machinery and equipment, means of transport excluding cars, buildings, land and improvement of the asset and therefore a symmetrical solution similar to what we propose. We assume that this project is the creation of a favourable and announced climate strategy for responsible development for entrepreneurs so that they take the risk of new investments, while at the same time mitigating them with the risk of reducing the tax burden. The expected effect of the change in revenue generated by foreign air navigation companies in Poland will be to remove the difficulties in applying the current regulation in practice, and to eliminate the excessive obligation will allow Polish economic operators to reduce tax risk, as well as to allocate resources to other activities. Estimated impact on the state budget and local government units. We assume that the revenue of the state budget will decrease mainly in the first 3 years of the regulation, but this reduction will decrease in the following years. We bear in mind that this decrease will be due to a different distribution of dues over time, not to the omission, payment or reduction of dues at all. Furthermore, we assume that the aim of the programme is not to reduce the charges, but to stimulate the supply side of the economy, the purchasing party for these machines and equipment, which should lead to an increase in the production potential of the economy and increase investment levels, especially in the small and medium-sized enterprises sector. All the macroeconomic analyses we have carried out, and we have done them in cooperation, e.g. with the Institute for Market, Consumption and Conjugation Research, show that the change introduced through investment stimulation will provide a significant demand boost. In particular, we assume that the result will be approx. 2800 new jobs, and therefore the tax base will be expanded, which will be the beneficiary, among others. local government units that will retain part of the income from PIT. Our estimates have allowed us to determine that, as I mentioned, the program will generate less than 3,000 extras. jobs, which will translate into an increase in revenue by about PLN 138 million, which will, on the other hand, result in an increase in budgetary revenues as well as an increase in income at the disposal of consumers. Changes in the scope of income tax collection due to revenues generated in Poland by foreign air navigation companies will result in changes in the revenue of the state budget. These revenues will decrease, but not by more than PLN 0.5 million per year, and therefore this is a trace change. We also assume that this expected impact on the activities of micro and small enterprises... In addition, it is a positive change, and thus one which will be able to apply after the approval of the Sejm from the beginning of this tax year, even though it enters into its duration. In line with the information that we have after consultation, also widely understood consultations with entrepreneurs, this will indeed affect the expansion of machinery parks, even small ones, in small and medium-sized enterprises, and thus will be a significant investment boost, reducing, somewhat reducing the costs of making such investments. The draft law, as I mentioned at the outset, is not covered by the provisions of European Union law on State aid and the proposed solution, because it is not selective, concerns everyone. We often say that it is a support instrument primarily for small and medium-sized ones, but rather we estimate it because of the range of interventions rather than the limits. In the event of a one-off depreciation, all taxpayers will have the right to benefit from this form in a given tax year. The proposed amendment is in line with European Union law. By presenting this amendment to the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act, I recommend that you adopt the proposed amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On the other hand, the SKOK National Bank has requested that the catalogue of investments covered by the proposed relief be extended to include fixed assets both manufactured by the taxpayer itself and not owned by the taxpayer. Leaving the provisions of the amendment unchanged significantly reduces the list of investments covered by the proposed relief, which in turn will contribute to the emergence of negative economic effects, which, of course, we want to avoid. The Modern Club considers the main idea of the bill to be worthwhile and that it can positively improve the situation of our entrepreneurs, but the comments made during the public consultation cannot be ignored. The project abolishes the obligation to pay 10% tax on air ticket revenues. Both proposals for solutions in the project are certainly a convenience for a group of entrepreneurs. We are not simplifying this right, which has been put forward and debated today. The project introduces new solutions for depreciation write-offs, which were regulated in the recent past, in 2016. At that time, I would remind you that one-off depreciation was accepted from the initial value of fixed assets up to an amount not exceeding the equivalent of 50,000 in the tax year. EUR for taxpayers in the first year of activity and small taxpayers whose income in the previous year did not exceed EUR 1.2 million. The new draft law introduces an artificial lower limit on the value of the asset subject to a write-down of 10 thousand, without giving the possibility of one-off depreciation of the applicable unit rate of 3,5 thousand. That would make it a lot easier to write down. This is justified, all the more so as lower investment purchases relate to small companies, i.e. this prudent investment. The Polish Crafts Association states that these incentives to invest will not receive about 550 thousand. enterprises. Another example of the complexity of the law is not to include a single deduction of fixed assets which are depreciable, used by the taxpayer under a financial lease agreement or from the so-called. groups of investments in foreign fixed assets. It is worth noting that the project in question will not reduce the payments to the state budget, but will only spread them over time, and the revenue of the state budget will decrease mainly in the first 3 years of this regulation. In conclusion, the draft law, despite its comments and reservations, deserves further debate in the Committee on Public Finance and the proposed one-off depreciation mechanism will certainly provide a good incentive for investment. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-21-sejm-44-2.u352	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on personal income tax together with the self amendment of 25 May 2017, print No 1613. In the Parliamentary draft law, the applicant proposes an amendment to the Personal Income Tax Act of 26 July 1991, as amended, Dz. U. z 2016 poz. 2032. The proposal concerns the amendment of the Article. Article 22 (2) (b) Point 9 (1) to (3) and repeal. 9a and 10a. The applicant also proposes that the Act should cover the revenue generated by the creators from 1 January 2017 and apply from 1 January 2018. In the current legal situation - amendment of 24 October 2012 - the PO-PSL coalition introduced from 2013 the provision that in the tax year the total cost of obtaining the revenue referred to in paragraph 1. Paragraph 9 (1) to (3) shall not exceed 1/2 of the amount constituting the upper limit of the first tax scale range referred to in Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. The annual limit for accounting for 50% of the flat-rate revenue costs therefore amounts to PLN 42 764. It should be noted that these amounts are calculated against income less the deduction by the payer of social security contributions during the month in question. These powers are now available to a wide group of representatives of 37 different professions, which form a widely defined group of creators. In practice, many people and occupations benefit from the possibility of accounting for 50% of revenue costs, including: Actors, journalists, writers, filmmakers, musicians, painters, sculptors, graphic designers, lecturers, architects, computer scientists, but also consultants, marketers or trainers. In order to benefit from 50% of the cost of obtaining revenue, a work must be created, which according to the Copyright Law and related rights is any expression of creative activity of an individual nature determined in any form. The proposed amendment would abolish reductions in the amount of PLN 42 764 in order to account for 50% of the cost of obtaining revenue in relation to the revenue of the creators, which would improve their financial situation, mainly artists and cultural creators. But should training, marketing or consulting activities also have such forms of taxation? The state's tax policy is created and implemented by the government. It is the government that should determine which activities of the creators should be covered by 50% at the expense of obtaining revenue and what creative activities are important for the Polish state, its development and innovation. There should be arrangements between the Ministry of Finance and Development, the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage and the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. The tax authorities are not authorised to interpret the Copyright Act and therefore to determine whether the person is the creator and whether the controlled work constitutes copyright. At the same time, I would like to point out that the proposed changes would reduce the revenue of the state budget from the PIT tax at more than PLN 100 million, which would reduce the share of PIT for local government units. At the same time, I would like to point out that if the law had been in force since January 2018 for revenue generated in 2018, it would have to be adopted and published by the end of 2017. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I request that the law and justice be rejected in full in the first reading of the parliamentary bill amending the Act on personal income tax, print No 1613.
